{"type":"tool_call","subtype":"completed","call_id":"tool_300d3d67-171d-4efb-b23d-c1ae5a8da21","tool_call":{"readToolCall":{"args":{"path":"/tmp/world-experiment/npdes-obligation-targeted-resolution-v1/20756046496ec6dae5490703b0b20eb2/documents/farmington/statement_of_basis.txt","offset":448,"limit":50},"result":{"success":{"content":"constant 1.548 cfs/MGD is used. The 4Q3 is 278.6 MGD and the harmonic mean low flow is\n715.8 MGD.\n\n                   e. Chlorine\n\nThe facility uses chlorine to control bacteria. The NMWQS for total residual chlorine (TRC) are\n11 ug/l for chronic and 19 ug/l for acute conditions. The NNWQS for TRC are 4000 ug/L for\ndomestic water supply, and primary/secondary human contact; 19 ug/L for aquatic & wildlife\nhabitat-acute; and, 11 ug/L for aquatic & wildlife habitat-chronic and livestock watering.\n\nSince acute conditions do not allow dilution; the limit must be met at end-of-pipe but chronic\nstandards do allow dilution, the permit shall use the most stringent WQS for the permit limit.\nThe following shows the calculations.\n\nThe critical dilution (CD) is calculated as follows:\n\n   CD = Qe ÷ [(FQa) + Qe]\n\nwhere:\nQe = facility effluent or design flow; 6.67 MGD\nQa = 4Q3; 278.6 MGD\nF = fraction of stream allowed for mixing; 1.0\n\n   CD = 6.67 ÷ [(1.0 × 278.6) + 6.67]\n   CD = 0.0234 or 2.34%\n\nThe in-stream TRC concentration after allowing for dilution is; 11 ug/l ÷ 0.0234 = 470 ug/l.\nSince this value is greater than the 19 ug/l end-of-pipe acute standard, the 19 ug/l is more\nstringent and will be more protective. The draft permit maintains the 19 ug/l end-of-pipe acute\nlimit since it is more stringent and will be more protective than the 11 ug/l dilution based chronic\nlimit. In addition to the 19 ug/l chemical specific limitation, the draft permit also maintains the\nnarrative limit for total residual chlorine shall be “No Measurable.” TRC shall be limited as\nfollows: “After dechlorination and prior to final disposal, the effluent shall contain NO\nMEASURABLE (TRC) at any time. NO MEASURABLE will be defined as no detectable\nconcentration of TRC as determined by any approved method established in 40 CFR Part 136.\nThe effluent limitation for TRC is the instantaneous maximum and cannot be averaged for\nreporting purposes. The maximum dechlorinated TRC shall be monitored daily by grab sample.\nTRC shall be measured within fifteen (15) minutes of sampling.\n\fPERMIT NO. Salinity impacts have been a major concern in the United\nStates and Mexico. The salinity of the Colorado River increases as it flows downstream. The\nColorado River has carried an average salt load of approximately 9 million tons annually past\nHoover Dam, the uppermost location at which numeric criteria have been established.
